| TM-LLM-025 | Agent config / response steering the utility LLM during agent checks | Medium | Agent checks (knowledge/evaluation/agent-checks.md) pass the agent's own prompt, generated cases, and agent responses to the utility LLM for analysis, case generation, and judging. All such text is untrusted and is wrapped as XML-tagged data with metacharacters escaped (`xml_escape`); output is parsed into fixed shapes (findings, cases, a judge verdict) with severity/count/length clamps. A steered judge can at worst mis-score an advisory, behavioral health check, which never gates save, publish, or version creation. | MITIGATED |
| TM-LLM-026 | Health check runs spending agent model budget | Medium | `trigger_agent_health_check` (knowledge/evaluation/agent-checks.md) runs real sessions on the agent's configured model. Bounded: a fixed small number of generated cases (≤6), bounded per-case concurrency, a per-case turn budget and timeout, and the standard per-turn iteration cap. The trigger requires `agent.manage` and the utility LLM to be configured. | MITIGATED |
| TM-LLM-027 | llm_judge policy prompt steering via agent-authored tool content | Medium | `llm_judge` checks send tool-call arguments and tool results to the utility LLM for evaluation. This content is agent/model-authored and could attempt to override the policy prompt. Mitigated: the policy prompt is always the system message; the evaluated content is a user message with a `Content:` prefix. Tool names are XML-escaped. The judge is instructed to return a fixed JSON schema; the response parser only reads the `verdict` and `reason` fields, discarding anything else. A steered judge fails open (`allow`), never allowing more permissive behavior than the baseline. | MITIGATED |
| TM-LLM-028 | llm_judge latency amplification per tool call | Low | Each tool call can invoke up to `MAX_JUDGE_CALLS_PER_INVOCATION` (4) judge requests, each bounded by `JUDGE_TIMEOUT` (10 s). Maximum added latency per tool call: 40 s. Judge calls are serialized within a single tool invocation hook. Timeouts fail open. | MITIGATED |
| TM-LLM-029 | Guardrail `mcp` check data egress and verdict steering via external endpoint | Medium | The guardrails `mcp` check (knowledge/execution/guardrails.md) sends a bounded excerpt of tool-call arguments / tool output to an external MCP guardrail endpoint chosen by the agent operator. Egress is explicit and operator-configured (the server is a scoped-MCP reference resolved per session/org, see TM-TOOL-022 for tenant scoping), documented on the check type, and the payload is capped at 2 000 bytes (UTF-8 char-boundary safe). The endpoint returns a fixed `{"verdict":...}` JSON shape; the parser reads only `verdict`/`reason` and discards anything else, and any failure (timeout, connection/tool error, missing/unparseable verdict, server-not-configured) fails open (`allow`), a hostile or broken endpoint can never make execution more permissive than the no-guardrail baseline, only block. | MITIGATED |
| TM-LLM-030 | Guardrail `moderation` output check: assistant-text egress and latency amplification | Low | The guardrails `moderation` check (EVE-573, knowledge/execution/guardrails.md) runs on the end-of-message output boundary, sending a bounded excerpt (≤ 4 000 bytes, UTF-8-safe) of the finalized assistant message to the **org's own configured utility LLM** for classification, the same provider the agent already uses, not a new third party, so no additional cross-tenant or new-vendor egress is introduced. The classifier returns a fixed `{"scores":{...}}` shape; the parser reads only numeric category scores and ignores anything else. Latency is bounded: at most `MAX_MODERATION_CALLS_PER_INVOCATION` (4) calls per finalized message, each capped by a 10 s timeout (TM-DOS). Any failure (timeout, LLM error, unparseable response, or no utility service configured) fails open (`allow`), a decisions-service outage can only ever block, never make output more permissive than the no-guardrail baseline. Cost flows through the utility-LLM accounting pipeline. | MITIGATED |
| TM-LLM-031 | Provider-native compact context disclosure, cross-model replay, or concurrent history loss | High | Native compact payloads are envelope-encrypted in `session_compaction_checkpoints`, size-bounded before encryption, redacted from `Debug`, and carried only over the authenticated internal worker RPC. Public events expose metrics and an opaque checkpoint id, never payload bytes. Checkpoints are keyed by exact provider, model, and format version; incompatible requests reconstruct from raw events. A monotonic source-sequence CAS prevents an older concurrent compaction from replacing a newer boundary, while messages committed after the winning boundary remain a raw suffix. Proactive and reactive native paths share that install/apply boundary; window and cost pressure (bounded cumulative usage plus saturating raw tool-result byte counts) share the same checkpoint path and require a non-trivial current prompt. A failed install leaves the request unchanged, and an output with no material token/byte reduction is neither installed nor reported as successful. Failed and ineffective proactive calls record a bounded process-lifetime retry watermark keyed by session/provider/model, source boundary, input pressure, and transcript lineage; another call requires at least 4,096 tokens and 5% growth, bounding invisible no-op spend without crossing rollback branches. Forks copy only checkpoints whose source boundary is included in the copied event prefix. | MITIGATED |
| TM-LLM-032 | Connection request options abused as an egress or credential-at-rest surface | Medium | A provider connection may carry custom HTTP headers plus a prompt-cache diagnostics opt-in (`settings.request_options`, `knowledge/foundations/providers.md`), settable only by an org admin with `provider.manage` on a non-managed connection. Bounded and validated server-side (`validate_request_options` in `crates/server/src/domains/providers/service.rs`): at most 16 headers, syntactically valid header names, values under 2 KB, and transport-owned names (`host`, `content-length`, `transfer-encoding`, `connection`, `upgrade`) rejected, so a header cannot repoint the SSRF-guarded connection at another virtual host (TM-API-013) or corrupt framing; drivers drop the same set again at merge time. Headers only decorate requests to the connection's already-validated base URL, they do not add a new egress destination. Values are stored in the `settings` JSONB **unencrypted**, unlike `credentials_encrypted` (TM-LLM-001), but provider API responses redact every value while retaining header names and non-secret diagnostics configuration: `provider.view` is a member-level permission and `provider.manage` is not, so an unredacted read would hand every org member a value only an admin could set. Writes fold a blank submitted value onto the stored one (`merge_request_options`), so an editor that round-trips a redacted read cannot silently erase a gateway credential. Diagnostics are an opt-in flag only; the provider's response payload is logged and carried verbatim without being interpreted. | **ACCEPTED** |
| TM-LLM-036 | Unstored credential check abused as an egress trigger or credential oracle | Medium | `POST /v1/providers/check-credentials` (`knowledge/foundations/providers.md`) probes a candidate provider key with a read-only `list_models` call before any provider row exists. It adds no capability an org admin lacks: `provider.manage` gates it exactly as create + `sync-models` do, the destination is driver-declared and any supplied `base_url` passes the same SSRF gate as create (TM-API-013), and the request rides the SSRF-guarded shared provider clients. The submitted credential is never persisted, never logged, and never echoed; failures are logged server-side and returned as fixed strings classified from `LlmErrorKind`, so the response distinguishes "key rejected" from "provider unreachable" without leaking the provider's response body (TM-LLM-003). Unauthenticated probing is impossible, and an authenticated admin could already drive the same outbound call by saving a provider and syncing models. The Settings provider forms call it on input rather than on a button press (`use-credential-check.ts`), so the same admin-authorized egress now fires from typing; a debounce plus stale-response drop bounds it to pauses in typing rather than keystrokes, and the caller, destination, and disclosure are unchanged. | MITIGATED |
| TM-LLM-037 | Decisions service key exposed through agent configuration, or confused with the agent-facing TypeSafe connection | High | The deployment decisions service (`knowledge/operations/decisions-service.md`) reads `UTILITY_TYPESAFE_API_KEY` (and the driver selectors `UTILITY_DECISION_DRIVER` / `UTILITY_DECISION_MODEL`; the `openai` driver reuses the deployment's `UTILITY_OPENAI_API_KEY`) from the process environment, is carried as a host service on `HostComposition`, and is threaded only into capability execution context (`ToolContext`, `PostGenerationOutputContext`). It is not stored in provider records, not exposed through model selection, and not accepted from session or agent config. The decision contract can name a model, but the platform's composition never sets one and no guardrail or agent config field reaches it, so the selectable knob is absent from every surface an agent can write. The agent-facing `jev` capability is a **separate** surface that resolves a user-scoped connection token (or a session secret), never the deployment key, so an agent cannot spend or read the platform credential. Both clients redact the key from `Debug`, and API error bodies are never surfaced verbatim because an upstream error can echo the request headers that carry it. The reverse direction is closed too: an org that sets `system_decisions: organization` has its guardrail `jev` and Slack relevance checks answered by its own decision default, and when that model is missing, disabled, or failing the check errors (fail open, or Slack silence) rather than falling back to the deployment key ([session wrapper](../../crates/core/src/system_decisions.rs), `system_decisions_follow_the_org_choice_and_never_fall_back`, `an_org_without_a_usable_model_stays_silent_instead_of_using_the_deployment`). | MITIGATED |
| TM-LLM-042 | External Foreman worker inherits supervisor or unrelated host credentials | High | Foreman's external-process boundary clears the child environment, restores a narrow set of non-secret operating variables, and forwards only the selected preset's explicit provider-key allowlist. Custom commands receive no environment credentials. In particular, the supervisor's `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` is never forwarded. A real-child-process regression covers the Codex, yolop, and custom-command policies with an inherited sentinel. | MITIGATED |
| TM-LLM-038 | Guardrail `jev` engine: content egress and verdict steering | Medium | A `llm_judge` or `moderation` check with `engine: "jev"` sends the same bounded stage excerpt (2 000 bytes at the tool seams, 4 000 at the output seam, UTF-8-safe) to the deployment's decisions service instead of the utility model: the same egress class, an operator-configured destination, no per-agent choice. With the org setting `system_decisions: organization` the destination is the org's own default decision model instead, an org-admin choice on the org's own account, reached through the bound Jev path (TM-LLM-047) with the session budget and usage ledger; agent and guardrail config still cannot name a model or account. Under the `llm` decision driver (`UTILITY_DECISION_DRIVER=llm`) the destination is the utility model's own provider, with the state fenced as data in the prompt and caller ids replaced by positional keys; a reply naming an option or level the question did not offer is an error (fail open), never a verdict. The inspected content is passed as request **state**, never as instructions, and every question states that the content is data being inspected; the policy itself is operator-authored and lives in the question. Steering cannot manufacture a verdict, because there is no verdict to write: the model returns a probability and the configured `threshold` decides in code, which also removes the utility-LLM path's unparseable-verdict fail-open. Latency is bounded by one batched request per stage under a 10 s timeout, capped at `MAX_DECISIONS_QUESTIONS` (24) questions (TM-DOS); the deployment client does not retry. Any failure (timeout, error, unanswered question, no decisions service configured) fails open (`allow`), so a decisions-service outage can only ever block less, never make execution more permissive than the no-guardrail baseline. | MITIGATED |
| TM-LLM-039 | Captured request body widens where a prompt can be written | Medium | `LlmCallConfig::capture_request` records the driver's serialized request body on `LlmCompletionMetadata::request_body` for evaluation and request-log consumers that would otherwise reconstruct an approximation. The body carries the whole prompt, so the capture is **off by default** and is a per-call decision by the caller that owns the destination. It is the request body only: authentication travels in headers and is never serialized into it (asserted in `crates/contracts/src/openai_protocol.rs` tests). Nothing in the runtime turns it on, and no API response or event carries the field today, so enabling it is what decides where the prompt lands. | **CALLER RISK** |
| TM-LLM-041 | User content forges an Anthropic in-place system message | High | Anthropic request layout tracks later system-message provenance in a private typed structure (`crates/drivers/drivers/src/anthropic/driver_layout.rs`) rather than encoding it in message text. Conversion promotes only indexes recorded while inspecting trusted `MessageRole::System` values; marker-like user strings remain user-role content byte-for-byte. Regression coverage includes both former NUL-prefixed sentinels across model families. | MITIGATED |
| TM-LLM-033 | Session transcript steers its own generated run summary, or the summary becomes an unbounded/uncosted LLM surface | Medium | Run summaries (EVE-867, `crates/server/src/services/run_summary.rs`) send run content to the **org-independent utility LLM**, the same footing as `AnalyzeAgent` (TM-LLM-030's provider reasoning), so no new vendor or cross-tenant egress is introduced and the spend is outside the session's budget and token counters. Transcript content is untrusted input: instructions live only in the system prompt and are never interpolated with run content, which is passed as a separate user message inside a labelled `RUN_DIGEST` fence with newlines flattened per event so no event can forge fence structure or address the model. The digest is bounded three ways — at most 120 trailing events, 400 bytes per event, 8 000 bytes total — and successful tool output (the bulkiest, least trustworthy content) is excluded entirely, only failures are carried. Output is bounded at 120 tokens and rejected above 400 characters, so a runaway generation is never stored. One call per terminal turn with a 30 s timeout and **no retry**: a failure costs a missing sentence, never a retry storm against a paid endpoint. Generation is spawned and awaited by nobody, so it cannot delay or fail a run, and it is skipped entirely for chat sources and when no utility LLM is configured. The stored value is rendered as text, never as markup. Residual risk is the summary sentence itself being attacker-influenced prose shown to an operator, which is why it is Medium rather than closed. | MITIGATED |
| TM-LLM-043 | An agent's loop runs on OpenAI's Agents API without the tenant opting in, sending conversation content, instructions, and tool results to a managed harness without Zero Data Retention, or a remote loop runs a tool outside Everruns' pipeline | High | Three gates: the `openai-agents-api` Cargo feature, the `openai_agents_api` org flag (internal by default, with platform-only enrolment; explicit grade overrides determine rollout authority) (the server strips the gated `openai_agents_api_runtime` capability from worker snapshots and rejects it on writes), and the capability itself. The endpoint is offered only for a provider bound to `api.openai.com`, using that provider's own credentials; gateways and other drivers fall back to the native loop. The backend sends every Everruns tool as a client function, so each call crosses the Act pipeline (approval gate, pre/post-tool guardrails, network access, durable claim); a configuration with OpenAI-hosted tools, direct MCP, provider subagents, or an environment is refused before the provider is called (`ensure_enforceable`, `ensure_runtime_policy`). The production driver also checks observed provider work in live events, saved snapshots before client execution, and final reconciliation: only the two known `codex` inventory listings with explicit empty arguments and completed empty inventories are tolerated. Nonempty, malformed, errored or unfinished listings, unknown/provider-hosted calls and subagent work durably fail the turn and request cancellation; client actions must name an offered function under this root with no subagent provenance. Fixed policy text never includes rejected provider payloads. A call the pipeline parks (approval, client-side tool, connection setup) parks the Everruns turn while the provider's required action stays open; only a recorded approval runs it again, under a fresh local id the gate re-checks, and a rejection, expiry, or missing decision submits a failure. A budget that is paused or exhausted stops the turn before more tools run. Output guardrails (streaming and end-of-message, including `jev` checks) judge every remote assistant message before it is recorded; a trip saves a policy stop, records only the replacement, and cancels the provider turn. Direct MCP, allowed only outside the production backend, needs HTTPS, an explicit non-empty allowlist, and no credentials, and MCP transport headers are redacted from `Debug` and dropped on import, so MCP credentials never reach the provider, logs, or an imported agent. Provider ids are path-validated before use. Projection never reads a `reasoning` item's hidden `content` or `encrypted_content` or a `compaction` item's encrypted context, so only the provider-curated reasoning summary reaches `reason.item` (TM-LLM-034); provider ids are correlation metadata beside, never instead of, local ids, and a turn terminal event ends only the root turn it names. Residual: observed provider tools have already started outside the Everruns gate; refusal and cancellation cannot undo their effects or prove the absence of ambient provider capabilities. Empty inventory output is evidence of an empty returned list only. The provider keeps the turn's text in its own session, and a guardrail cannot undo a side effect a tool already had. See [OpenAI Agents API runtime](../execution/openai-agents-api-runtime.md#policy-at-the-tool-and-output-boundaries). | MITIGATED (partial; provider execution residual accepted) |
| TM-LLM-044 | Conversation content held in an OpenAI Agents API session outlives the Everruns session that created it (deletion, retention, a replaced provider session), a fork or import presents a session without its provider-held context as a faithful copy, or lifecycle work leaks the provider credential | Medium | Every path that drops a provider session id (session delete and every cascade into `agents_api_sessions`, replacement, retention release) records a tombstone in the same transaction through a trigger (`agents_api_provider_deletions`, migration 156), so a crash or a provider outage cannot lose the deletion. The server's lifecycle task deletes the remote session with the owning provider's current key, resolved at delete time and sent only to `api.openai.com` (`official_endpoint`); 404 counts as deleted, so retries converge; failures back off and record a stable code, never a response body. Tombstones and the checkpoint's plaintext columns hold the provider session id and the Everruns provider id, never a credential. `AGENTS_API_SESSION_RETENTION_DAYS` releases idle provider sessions without touching the Everruns record. Forks (including detached spawns seeded as forks) of a session with a checkpoint are refused with `agents_api_session_not_forkable`; no endpoint imports a provider session. Permanent provider failures end the turn with Everruns-authored messages and stable codes (`provider_misconfigured`, `model_unavailable`, `provider_quota_exhausted`, `provider_session_unavailable`), so a provider body that echoes part of a key never reaches a turn. Residual: Everruns cannot verify OpenAI's own deletion or backups; a session whose provider or organization was deleted first, or whose key moved to another OpenAI project, cannot be deleted and is left to OpenAI's retention (tombstone kept as `failed`). See [OpenAI Agents API runtime](../execution/openai-agents-api-runtime.md#session-lifecycle). | MITIGATED |
| TM-LLM-045 | The opt-in Responses WebSocket transport reaches an internal host through an org-configured base URL, or a parked socket carries one credential's turn under another | High | The transport is offered only for `api.openai.com` on the OpenAI driver and only when a call or provider opts in. The dial resolves the host and refuses it when any address is private or internal (the EVE-623 guard of the shared HTTP clients), connects to the resolved addresses, and treats any handshake other than `101` as a failed dial, so redirects are never followed. Parked sockets are keyed by a hash of the URL and every handshake header, credentials included, plus the exact response id being continued, and a checkout is exclusive, so a socket never serves another key, session, or concurrent request. Handshake headers are marked sensitive. Pinned by `websocket_transport_is_limited_to_api_openai_com`, `blocked_hostnames_are_refused_before_connecting` and `identity_separates_credentials_and_ignores_header_order`. See [OpenAI Responses WebSocket Transport](../foundations/openai-responses-websocket.md#security). | MITIGATED |
| TM-LLM-046 | A transcript seeded into a new OpenAI Agents API session re-enters earlier assistant text and tool output as user-role content, so injected text in a tool result gains user authority, forges turns, or crowds out the new session's context | Medium | Seeding (EVE-1146) applies only when a turn creates a provider session, and the provider's create `input` admits user-role messages only, so earlier turns cannot keep their roles. The transcript is a separate user message from the turn's own input, opened by a framing header that calls it a record of earlier turns, context and not instructions, whose tool calls already ran; entries are JSON lines with `<` and `>` escaped, so no entry can close the fence or start another, and system messages, unanswered calls, attachment bytes, reasoning, and provider-opaque content are never included (TM-LLM-034). Assistant text is taken from the record after Everruns output guardrails. Bounded to the newest 200 entries and 32 KiB, each text 4 KiB (`crates/core/src/host/openai_agents_api/seed.rs`); all of it was already sent to the same provider under the same credentials when the conversation happened, so no new egress. Residual: the model can still follow an instruction inside a recorded tool output, as with any tool output (cf. TM-LLM-007), and every client function it calls in response still crosses Everruns' tool pipeline (TM-LLM-043). | MITIGATED |
| TM-LLM-047 | Image provider base URL sends the decrypted provider key or requests to internal or unapproved hosts | High | Image tools, catalog-bound Jev decisions, and org-selected guardrail and Slack decisions build no HTTP client of their own. [Bound Jev](../../crates/integrations/src/typesafe/bound.rs) resolves an exact enabled model/account per call, rejects foreign or wrong-service selections, checks budgets and emits usage without secrets or state. The `gpt_image_gen` capability (`crates/integrations/src/openai_image/src/images.rs`): generation and edit requests, buffered and streamed, go through `ToolContext.egress_service` with the session `network_access` ACL and `require_dns_pinning()`, so the host system allowlist applies, the hostname is resolved inside the egress boundary, every answer must be public, and the connection is pinned to those addresses. The egress transport never follows redirects and the client treats any 3xx as an error, so the key never leaves the validated origin. Credentials and base URL are re-resolved per call, so an edited base URL with a retained key is re-validated. No egress service means the tools fail closed. Static registration checks (`url_validation::validate_safe_url`) remain a first filter only. Covered by `rebound_dns_to_internal_address_is_refused_before_connect`, `redirects_are_not_followed_and_key_is_not_forwarded`, and `changed_base_url_with_retained_key_is_revalidated_per_call` (EVE-1174) | MITIGATED |
| TM-LLM-049 | Slack relevance check for a message with no session yet runs an org decision model outside any budget or ledger | Low | With `system_decisions: organization`, a Slack message that would start a session is decided before one exists. [Session-less execution](../../crates/server/src/api/slack_events/org_decisions.rs) resolves the org's default decision model without a session, so personal providers fail closed; the call uses host runtime egress with `require_dns_pinning()` and a 2 s overall deadline; there is no session budget to check, so usage is written to a structured log line (model, provider, tokens, cost, success) instead of a session ledger. Exposure is one bounded Noul question per unmentioned message on an endpoint set to Relevant messages, on the org's own account. A thread that already has a session is decided on that session's budget and ledger. Any failure is silence. | ACCEPTED |

### Mitigation Details

**TM-LLM-001, Key Retrieval Flow:**
```
Worker resolves model and provider
    → gRPC GetTurnContext returns credential-free model + provider identity
    → authenticated provider RPC resolves that exact provider under the turn org
    → control plane decrypts the provider credential
    → worker composes non-serializable runtime Provider over protocol driver
    → ProviderAuth resolves headers/signing per request attempt
    → LLM API call over HTTPS
    → provider dropped with the bounded execution context
```

Workers never have direct database access or encryption keys. The private
durable-engine entry owns turn/workflow wiring and reaches PostgreSQL through
durable's store API. Database-driver isolation checks normal, build, optional,
and renamed dependency edges; embedded hosts also open SQLite through durable,
while retaining their existing schemas and shared query handles. Contracts'
optional typed-id SQLx codecs grant no connection ownership. See
[`check-database-driver-isolation.sh`](../../scripts/lib/check-database-driver-isolation.sh)
and [`check-durable-isolation.sh`](../../scripts/lib/check-durable-isolation.sh).
Credentials do
not enter model records, internal model DTOs, events, logs, or serializable
runtime values.

**TM-LLM-007, Prompt Injection (ACCEPTED):**
Indirect prompt injection via tool results or user messages is an inherent LLM limitation. Mitigations:
- Role separation (system, user, assistant, tool_result)
- Max iteration limit prevents infinite loops
- No automatic code execution without registered tool
- Monitoring via usage tracking

**TM-LLM-021, Utility LLM Service:**
The utility LLM exists for capability internals only. It is configured from the
host environment, defaults to disabled, fixes the model in code, and reaches
capabilities through `ToolContext` rather than through agent model/provider
configuration.

**TM-LLM-037, Decisions Service:**
The decision service has the same posture as the utility LLM and for the same
reasons: host environment configuration, disabled by default, reachable only
through capability execution context. Model choice differs in mechanism, not in
effect: the contract carries an optional model name so an embedding application
can pin a version, and the platform pins its own by never setting it and never
exposing a config field that could. The agent-writable surface has no model knob
either way. It is a second service rather than a mode of the first because its
answers are values, not text — which is what removes the guardrail fail-open on
an unparseable verdict.

## 9. Durable Execution Engine (TM-DURABLE)

| ID | Threat | Severity | Mitigation | Status |
|----|--------|----------|------------|--------|
| TM-DURABLE-001 | Task hijacking | High | Ownership verified on completion; late finisher gets `TaskNotOwned` error | MITIGATED |
| TM-DURABLE-002 | gRPC unauthenticated access | High | Bearer token auth via `WORKER_GRPC_AUTH_TOKEN` (required in production); optional mTLS via `WORKER_GRPC_TLS_*` env vars; workers are cross-org by design | MITIGATED |
| TM-DURABLE-003 | Event injection | Medium | Events created via gRPC only; validated for session membership | MITIGATED |
| TM-DURABLE-004 | Queue flooding | Medium | Per-workflow pending task limit (default 100, configurable via `MAX_PENDING_TASKS_PER_WORKFLOW`) | MITIGATED |
| TM-DURABLE-005 | Heartbeat timeout manipulation | Low | 30s timeout is reasonable for LLM operations; reclaimed tasks re-queued | MITIGATED |
| TM-DURABLE-006 | Dead letter queue growth | Low | Failed tasks preserved in DLQ; no automatic cleanup | **ACCEPTED** |
| TM-DURABLE-007 | Task state manipulation | Medium | Tasks immutable after creation; only status transitions allowed via state machine | MITIGATED |
| TM-DURABLE-008 | Worker impersonation | High | Bearer token auth + optional mTLS prevents unauthorized access (see TM-DURABLE-002) | MITIGATED |
| TM-DURABLE-009 | Replay attack on workflow events | Low | Event store is append-only; events processed in sequence order | MITIGATED |
| TM-DURABLE-010 | Durable API endpoints accessible to ordinary tenant users | High | All `/v1/durable/*` endpoints require explicit platform-user auth; `ResolvedOrg -> Caller` preserves `is_platform_user` for policy/config evaluation | MITIGATED |
| TM-DURABLE-011 | Presigned image URL forgery | Medium | HMAC-SHA256 signed with `WORKER_GRPC_AUTH_TOKEN`; 5-min expiry; signature covers image_id + org_id + expires; constant-time comparison | MITIGATED |

### Mitigation Details

**TM-DURABLE-001, Task Ownership:**
```
Worker A claims task → heartbeat timeout → task reclaimed by Worker B
Worker A finishes late → CompleteDurableTask → TaskNotOwned error
Worker B continues execution → task completes correctly
```
Prevents duplicate activity execution when workers lose connectivity.

**TM-DURABLE-002, gRPC Security (MITIGATED):**
Workers authenticate to control plane gRPC (port 9001) via two layered mechanisms:

1. **Bearer token auth** (`WORKER_GRPC_AUTH_TOKEN` env var), required in production (server panics on startup if unset in non-dev mode)
   - Server: `GrpcAuthInterceptor` validates `authorization: Bearer <token>` on every request
   - Client: `GrpcClientAuth` injects the bearer token into every outgoing request
2. **Mutual TLS (mTLS)**: optional, configured via `WORKER_GRPC_TLS_*` env vars
   - Server presents its certificate (`WORKER_GRPC_TLS_CERT`/`WORKER_GRPC_TLS_KEY`) and verifies client certs against `WORKER_GRPC_TLS_CA_CERT`
   - Worker presents its client certificate and verifies the server against the CA
   - Provides encryption + mutual identity verification at the transport layer
   - Bearer token auth remains active as defense-in-depth even when mTLS is enabled

**Design decision:** Workers are intentionally cross-org. They are stateless task executors that process work from any organization's queue. Org-scoping is enforced at the application layer (HTTP API), not the internal gRPC transport.

**TM-DURABLE-010, Durable API Endpoints (MITIGATED):**
All `/v1/durable/*` HTTP endpoints require explicit platform-user auth. The auth backend returns `AuthUser.is_platform_user`, HTTP caller construction preserves it through `ResolvedOrg`, and `/v1/durable/config` exposes the same policy result for UI gating.

## 10. Scheduled Tasks (TM-SCHED)

| ID | Threat | Severity | Mitigation | Status |
|----|--------|----------|------------|--------|
| TM-SCHED-001 | Malicious schedule creation / resource abuse | Medium | Only platform users can create or manage durable schedules; schedule channels enforce a minimum cron interval (`SCHEDULE_CHANNEL_MIN_INTERVAL_SECONDS`, default 300 s) and an org-level cap on enabled schedule channels (`SCHEDULE_CHANNEL_MAX_PER_ORG`, default 10). Agent-created **session schedules** (`create_schedule` / `spawn_background` with a `schedule` arg), where each fire dispatches a real worker turn, enforce the same shape worker-side at create time: per-session cap (`MAX_ACTIVE_SCHEDULES_PER_SESSION`, 5), per-org cap on active schedules (`RESOURCE_LIMIT_MAX_SESSION_SCHEDULES_PER_ORG`, default 100, so unlimited sessions cannot imply unlimited schedules per org), and a minimum recurring cron interval (`SESSION_SCHEDULE_MIN_INTERVAL_SECONDS`, default 300 s). Per-org count is `org_id`-scoped (no cross-tenant leakage); SaaS tunes the caps per plan via env. | MITIGATED |
| TM-SCHED-002 | Catch-up explosion on restart | High | `max_catch_up` limits catch-up runs (default: 1); prevents hundreds of executions on restart | MITIGATED |
| TM-SCHED-003 | Concurrent execution overload | Medium | `max_concurrent` field enforced; trigger skipped if limit reached | MITIGATED |
| TM-SCHED-004 | Invalid cron expression DoS | Low | Cron parser validates expression at creation time; invalid expressions rejected | MITIGATED |
| TM-SCHED-005 | Scheduler crash leaves tasks untriggered | Medium | Durable execution ensures tasks are created; if executor crashes, tasks auto-reclaimed via heartbeat | MITIGATED |
| TM-SCHED-006 | Embedded schedule duplication, starvation, or stranding after runner failure | Medium | `everruns::local` claims due occurrences in an immediate SQLite transaction, scopes claims by org and the host's current routable-session snapshot before ordering/limiting, heartbeats claims during host delivery, and reclaims only stale leases. Successful delivery advances or disables the stored occurrence; failure remains durable and uses the claim timeout as retry cooldown. The external `send_message` boundary is at-least-once: a process crash after host acceptance but before completion commit can retry, so embedded hosts must tolerate duplicate scheduled prompts in that narrow window. Batch size and route-filter parameters are bounded by the configured batch size and the host's active routes. | MITIGATED |
| TM-SCHED-007 | A same-org caller denied session access by a custom `PermissionResolver` reads, changes, deletes, or fires a session's schedules | High | Every `/v1/sessions/{session_id}/schedules` route evaluates the session policy with the configured resolver before any schedule lookup: list/get require `SESSION_VIEW`, update/delete/trigger require `SESSION_MANAGE`; a refusal is a 403 that carries no schedule contents. Org scoping and the parent-session match still apply after the policy check. On a Platform Chat session, update/delete/trigger also require the persisted session owner (TM-AGENT-017) and answer anyone else with a 403; reads stay unbound, like session reads (EVE-1183). See `crates/server/src/api/session_schedules.rs`; covered by `contracts::session_schedules_policy_test`. (EVE-1179) | MITIGATED |

## 10b. Trigger Events (TM-TRIGGER)

Every event that reaches an agent trigger (schedule fire, webhook request, and later GitHub and MCP events) passes through one pipeline: filter, deduplicate, route to a session, dispatch. See `crates/server/src/domains/agent_triggers/events.rs` and `knowledge/runtime-resources/agent-triggers.md`.

| ID | Threat | Severity | Mitigation | Status |
|----|--------|----------|------------|--------|
| TM-TRIGGER-001 | A redelivered or replayed event starts a second run and spends budget twice | Medium | When the source supplies an event id (a webhook trigger's `event_id_template`, a GitHub delivery id), the delivery row claims it under a partial unique index on `(trigger_id, event_id)`; a later event with the same id is recorded as `duplicate` and starts nothing, including under concurrent requests. A failed delivery releases the claim so a source retry can still run. Sources without an id (schedules, webhooks without a template) are not deduplicated. | MITIGATED |
| TM-TRIGGER-002 | A busy or hostile source grows the delivery history without bound | Medium | Every event, including filtered and duplicate ones, is recorded, so the pipeline prunes each trigger's history to its newest `DELIVERY_HISTORY_LIMIT` rows after each event. Stored event ids, subjects and reasons are truncated to 512 bytes. Webhook ingress still authenticates and rate-limits before any row is written. | MITIGATED |
| TM-TRIGGER-003 | Attacker-controlled event text (a repository name, a title) becomes a session routing key | Low | `per_thread` sessions are keyed by a SHA-256 digest of the rendered subject, never the text itself, so event text cannot collide with or spoof reserved session tags; the subject is shown only as display text in the session title and delivery log. | MITIGATED |
| TM-TRIGGER-004 | Events the agent should ignore still start runs | Low | Trigger filters are evaluated before a session is created or a message is dispatched; a miss is recorded as `filtered` with the failing condition and costs no model call. | MITIGATED |
| TM-TRIGGER-005 | Forged, stale, replayed or oversized MCP event deliveries start runs on an `mcp_event` trigger | High | The callback `/v1/e/{ingress_id}/mcp-events` carries no user auth, so the Standard Webhooks signature, keyed by a per-subscription secret Everruns generates and stores encrypted, is the only gate: it is checked in constant time before the body is parsed, a timestamp more than five minutes off is refused, and the verification challenge is echoed only when signed. Bodies over 256 KiB are refused unread. `webhook-id` is the event id, so a replay is a recorded duplicate (TM-TRIGGER-001). Unknown, disabled, deleted, flagged-off and superseded subscriptions answer `410 Gone`, which ends them at the server even when `events/unsubscribe` was lost; re-enabling issues a new secret, so an old one stops working. Outbound `events/*` calls go through the host egress service with the attachment's own credential, and `actsAs: user` attachments are refused. Covered by `crates/server/tests/domain/mcp_event_triggers_test.rs`. | MITIGATED |

## 11. Observability (TM-OBS)

| ID | Threat | Severity | Mitigation | Status |
|----|--------|----------|------------|--------|
| TM-OBS-001 | PII in Braintrust events | Medium | Full messages + LLM completions sent to Braintrust; customer responsibility to enable/disable | **CALLER RISK** |
| TM-OBS-002 | API keys in OTel spans | Medium | Spans are built from persisted events, which never carry provider credentials (TM-LLM-004); by default they carry only ids, model and provider names, token counts, timing, tool names, and error classes | MITIGATED |
| TM-OBS-010 | Prompts, completions, reasoning, and tool payloads in OTel spans | Medium | Content capture is off by default and only enabled by the operator through `OTEL_INSTRUMENTATION_GENAI_CAPTURE_MESSAGE_CONTENT` (`crates/core/src/host/observability/otel_config.rs`, marker TM-OBS-010). When on, instructions, messages, tool definitions and descriptions, tool arguments/results (including hook-rewritten `everruns.tool.executed_arguments`, exported as `tool.completed` recorded them: bounded and credential-redacted, TM-HOOK-007), and reasoning text go to the configured OTLP endpoint (TM-OBS-003) in the Gen-AI and OpenInference attribute shapes; image bytes are never copied. When off, tool spans retain the non-sensitive tool name and call identifier for correlation. Enabling it is the operator's retention decision, as with Braintrust (TM-OBS-001) | **CALLER RISK** |
| TM-OBS-003 | OTLP endpoint compromise | Medium | OTLP endpoint is configurable; must be trusted internal infrastructure | **CALLER RISK** |
| TM-OBS-004 | Braintrust API key exposure | Medium | Key stored in env var; not logged | MITIGATED |
| TM-OBS-005 | Log injection | Low | Structured logging via `tracing` crate; no raw string interpolation in log output | MITIGATED |
| TM-OBS-006 | Sensitive data in error logs | Medium | Errors logged server-side only; API keys and passwords excluded from tracing fields | MITIGATED |
| TM-OBS-013 | NATS credentials exposed through startup or connection-error logs | High | Userinfo is removed from `NATS_URL` before the configured servers are logged, including malformed and comma-separated values. Parse errors use fixed context rather than interpolating configuration, so full-chain warning logs cannot recover credentials. Covered by `nats::tests::server_urls_are_redacted_for_logs` and `invalid_url_error_does_not_include_credentials`. | MITIGATED |
| TM-OBS-007 | No security audit logging | Medium | Structured audit_logs table with fire-and-forget writes for auth events and for agent-domain events (app invocations, soft-approval asks and grants, see TM-OBS-011); admin-only query API | MITIGATED |
| TM-OBS-008 | Raw trajectory content leak via dataset export | High | Dataset export (`POST /v1/evals/{eval_id}/runs/{run_id}/dataset` enqueues; `GET …/dataset/{dataset_id}` fetches, `knowledge/evaluation/dataset-export.md`) exports raw model-view message content, so both endpoints are gated by the dedicated `DATASET_EXPORT` policy (`dataset.export`, requires `OrgAgentsManage` + `OrgSessionsManage`) distinct from read-only `EVAL_VIEW`/`REPORT_VIEW`; org-scoped through `get_run` (cross-org runs are 404, see TM-TENANT-001/002) and `get_dataset` re-resolves the run and verifies the handle's `eval_run_id` before returning, so a guessed dataset id from another org/run is 404 (covered by `test_dataset_export_cross_org_returns_not_found`); always-on secret scrubbing strips credential patterns from every exported string before it is persisted; optional `redact_content` blanks message/tool content while preserving structure. Phase 2 adds an at-rest store: the scrubbed NDJSON is persisted on the org-scoped `eval_run_datasets` row (never raw un-scrubbed content), and the row's FK to `eval_runs` is `ON DELETE SET NULL` so deleting the run detaches its datasets | MITIGATED |
| TM-OBS-011 | Session conversation text widened to org admins through approval audit rows | Low | `soft_approval` grants are copied from the session event log into `audit_logs` by `ApprovalAuditListener` (`crates/server/src/services/approval_audit.rs`), which `AUDIT_LOG_VIEW` exposes to org admins who may hold no access to the session itself. The copied fields are model-authored from the conversation and can carry whatever a participant pasted; the tool descriptions instruct the model not to pass secrets, which is guidance, not enforcement. Each free-text detail is bounded to 512 characters with an explicit truncation marker (`MAX_DETAIL_CHARS`, marker TM-OBS-007), so the row carries an excerpt plus the turn/message correlation ids rather than the full text, and a model emitting a huge argument cannot write an unbounded row. The complete text remains only in the session event log under session permissions. Actor and `org_id` are resolved server-side from the session and its authenticated `input.message` initiator, never from tool arguments, so a model cannot forge either. | MITIGATED |
| TM-OBS-012 | Service-owned MCP activity is recorded as the invoking user or accepts a forged acting principal | Medium | Before durable tool-started and tool-completed events are written, the event service resolves the effective scoped MCP attachment and the session's agent-identity principal under the session org. For `actsAs: service`, it overwrites `acting_principal_id` with that principal and preserves a supplied human `initiator_principal_id`; when the initiator is absent it records the session owner. Missing principals and session, agent, configuration, or principal lookup failures prevent event persistence. Event metadata contains principal ids only, never OAuth credentials. | MITIGATED |
| TM-OBS-009 | Audit-log client IP spoofing through forwarding headers | Low | Audit IP attribution uses the same trusted-proxy extractor as auth rate limiting: forwarding headers are honored only from trusted peers and `X-Forwarded-For` is peeled from the trusted right side instead of trusting the client-controlled leftmost entry. Direct untrusted peers record the socket peer address. | MITIGATED |
| TM-OBS-014 | Tool narration exposes credentials or private payloads in work-log previews | Low | Tool owners select display labels explicitly; shared [narration presentation](../../crates/contracts/src/runtime/tool_narration/action.rs) bounds previews, removes controls and strips URL credentials, queries and fragments even when their values contain quotes or parentheses. Generic fallback does not inspect arbitrary bodies. Outgoing message previews use the existing session permission boundary; internal prompts, file contents and typed computer text are omitted. | MITIGATED |

### Mitigation Details

**TM-OBS-001, Braintrust Data Flow:**
```
Agent turn → events emitted → BraintrustEventListener (async)
    → Convert to OpenAI format
    → POST /v1/project_logs/{project_id}/insert
    → Fire-and-forget (no retry)
```
Full conversation data (user messages, LLM responses, tool results) is transmitted. Organizations must evaluate whether Braintrust integration is appropriate given their data classification requirements.

**TM-OBS-007, Security Audit Logging (MITIGATED):**
- `audit_logs` PostgreSQL table (migration 005) stores structured events with: org_id, actor_id, event_type, ip_address, metadata, created_at.
- Event types follow `domain.action.outcome` convention: `auth.login.success`, `auth.login.failure`, `auth.register.success`, `auth.token_refresh.success`, `auth.personal_access_token.created`, `auth.personal_access_token.deleted`, `auth.oauth.success`, `auth.oauth.failure`.
- Fire-and-forget writes via `auth::audit::emit()`, audit failures never block auth operations.
- Admin-only query API: `GET /v1/organizations/:org_id/audit-logs` with filters for event_type, actor_id, cursor pagination.
- Retention: `delete_audit_logs_before()` method available for scheduled cleanup.

## 12. Web Security (TM-WEB)

| ID | Threat | Severity | Mitigation | Status |
|----|--------|----------|------------|--------|
| TM-WEB-001 | XSS via stored content | Medium | React UI auto-escapes; file preview uses Shiki (no raw HTML injection) | MITIGATED |
| TM-WEB-002 | CSRF on state-changing requests | Medium | SameSite=Lax cookies; JSON content type required; no GET side effects | MITIGATED |
| TM-WEB-003 | Cookie theft via XSS | High | Refresh token cookie: HTTP-only; access token cookie: HTTP-only | MITIGATED |
| TM-WEB-004 | Clickjacking | Medium | X-Frame-Options: DENY and CSP frame-ancestors: 'none' via SetResponseHeaderLayer | MITIGATED |
| TM-WEB-005 | Missing security headers | Low | CSP, X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff, Referrer-Policy, Permissions-Policy via tower-http middleware | MITIGATED |
| TM-WEB-006 | Open redirect in OAuth flow | Medium | OAuth callbacks validated against configured redirect URIs | MITIGATED |
| TM-WEB-007 | CORS wildcard exposure | Medium | `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` not set by default; must be explicitly configured | MITIGATED |
| TM-WEB-008 | Open redirect via login page `return_to` or request-controlled login origin | Medium | `sanitizeReturnTo` (`apps/ui/src/lib/auth-redirect.ts`) restricts `return_to` to relative paths: must start with `/`, never `//` (protocol-relative), never `/\` (browser-normalized), never an absolute URL, and no ASCII controls that browsers could remove to create a protocol-relative destination. The optional remote login destination comes only from trusted `AUTH_LOGIN_ORIGIN` configuration, is validated at server startup as an HTTP(S) origin with no credentials/path/query/fragment, and is exposed read-only via `/v1/auth/config`; no request or query value can select it. UI middleware reads only the deployment environment. Configured absolute login URLs use full-page navigation, never the client router. CLI and MCP OAuth emit only relative `return_to` paths. See `knowledge/security/authentication.md` "Login Page Contract". | MITIGATED |
| TM-WEB-A2UI-01 | XSS via `javascript:`/`data:` URL in A2UI `open_url` action or `Image.src` | High | A2UI JSON is LLM-emitted. `isSafeUrl` in `apps/ui/src/components/chat/a2ui-renderer.tsx` restricts action URLs and image sources to `http:`/`https:`/`mailto:` schemes; `window.open` also uses `noopener,noreferrer`. React auto-escapes all text props. See `knowledge/ui/a2ui.md`. | MITIGATED |
| TM-WEB-009 | XSS via SVG file preview (`<script>`, `on*` handlers, `javascript:` URLs, `<foreignObject>` HTML) | High | `SVGPreview` (`apps/ui/src/components/files/file-previews.tsx`) renders SVG inside an `<iframe sandbox="" srcDoc=...>` carrying a strict CSP meta tag (`default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; img-src data:`). Empty `sandbox` denies all flags (scripts, forms, popups, top-nav, same-origin); CSP is defense-in-depth. SVG bytes are NOT sanitized server-side, the gate is the iframe boundary. `getPreviewType` routes `.svg` to this path for both `text` and `base64` encodings; no `<img src=data:image/svg+xml>` path remains. Regression tests in `apps/ui/src/__tests__/file-previews.test.tsx` exercise script, on-handler, javascript-URL, and foreignObject payloads. See EVE-389. | MITIGATED |
| TM-WEB-010 | Credential/DOM theft or forced navigation via HTML file preview (user-supplied `.html`/`.htm` that runs JS) | High | In every mode the previewed document runs in an opaque origin (iframe `sandbox` WITHOUT `allow-same-origin`): `document.cookie`, `localStorage`, and `parent.document` all throw `SecurityError` (verified), and omitting `allow-top-navigation`/`allow-forms`/`allow-popups`/`allow-modals` blocks redirects, form posts, popups, and dialogs. **Server-backed mode** (file viewer): the iframe `src` loads `GET /v1/workspaces/{id}/fs/_/preview/{path}` (`workspace_files::preview_path`, HTML-only), whose response sets `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox allow-scripts; default-src 'none'; script-src 'unsafe-inline' 'unsafe-eval' data: blob:; …; connect-src 'none'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'none'` and `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN` (`session_files::sandboxed_html_response`). A network response does not inherit the app's `script-src 'self'`, so JS runs in the opaque sandbox; auth cookies authorize the fetch but the rendered DOM has no access to them, and the preview CSP denies network fetches so untrusted script cannot exfiltrate the previewed document to remote endpoints. **Static fallback** (`HtmlPreview` `srcDoc`, e.g. initial-files preview): the `about:srcdoc` document inherits the app's `script-src 'self'` so inline scripts do NOT run (verified under the real CSP, a child meta cannot loosen it); CSS/markup render and a hardening CSP meta (`object-src`/`base-uri`/`form-action 'none'`) is injected before user content, including `<header>` and custom-element fragments. Regression tests in `apps/ui/src/__tests__/file-previews.test.tsx`; manual verification under the real app CSP. | MITIGATED |
| TM-WEB-011 | Malicious PDF file preview (embedded JS, `/Launch`/`/URI` actions, mislabeled HTML) | Medium | `PdfPreview` (`apps/ui/src/components/files/file-previews.tsx`) renders via `<iframe src="data:application/pdf;base64,…">`. Chromium disables its PDF viewer inside *any* sandboxed iframe (verified for `data:` and `blob:` across sandbox flag combinations), so `sandbox` is unavailable; security comes from the `data:` URL's opaque origin (no cookie/DOM access), the out-of-process PDF viewer (PDF JS cannot script the host page), and the forced `application/pdf` type (mislabeled HTML is parsed as a broken PDF, never executed). Parent CSP carries `frame-src 'self' data:` to permit the frame. | MITIGATED |
| TM-WEB-012 | XSS via `javascript:`/`data:` URI in a citation source (`TextAnnotation.source.uri`) rendered as a link | High | Citation source URIs are LLM/retrieval-sourced (from `search_index`/`search_knowledge` tool results, `knowledge/runtime-resources/citations.md`). `isLinkableUri` in `apps/ui/src/components/chat/message-citations.tsx` renders a citation as a clickable `<a href>` only when the URI matches `^https?://`; every other scheme (`github://`, `everruns://`, or a hostile `javascript:`/`data:`) renders as inert text. `title`/`snippet`/`uri` are React text children (auto-escaped); the answer text passes through Streamdown with only internal `#cite-n` markers injected (real model links still route through `MarkdownLink`, which rejects `javascript:`). Links use `rel="noopener noreferrer"`. Mirrors TM-WEB-A2UI-01. | MITIGATED |
| TM-WEB-013 | Browser agent uses WebMCP as a confused deputy for unauthorized or unintended actions | High | WebMCP registrations exist only in the authenticated app behind deployment and org-effective gates; callbacks call the ordinary org-scoped APIs, revalidate their route/org binding at execution time, and every mutating or billable action pauses for a visible Everruns confirmation. Browser annotations are advisory only. | MITIGATED |
| TM-WEB-014 | Cross-origin frame discovers or invokes Everruns WebMCP tools | High | The UI document's Permissions Policy is `tools=(self)` when enabled and `tools=()` otherwise. Registrations never set `exposedTo`, so the browser's same-origin default prevents a cross-origin document from discovering or invoking them. | MITIGATED |
| TM-WEB-015 | Prompt injection or sensitive-data disclosure through WebMCP search/context results | High | Search is lazy and org-scoped, caps result count and output size, omits descriptions/prompts/transcripts/credentials, and marks returned content with `untrustedContentHint`. Context exposes only current navigation/resource metadata. | MITIGATED |
| TM-WEB-016 | Stale WebMCP callback acts after route, resource, org, or auth change | High | Every registration is tied to an `AbortSignal`; route/resource/org/auth changes abort it, pending confirmations are rejected, and callbacks compare their captured binding with current state before calling an API. In the adopted Chat split view only the conversation selected by the route registers session mutations; resolving a thread unregisters them and rejects callbacks until it is reopened. | MITIGATED |
| TM-WEB-017 | Browser retries duplicate a non-idempotent WebMCP mutation | Medium | Mutation annotations declare non-idempotence, every invocation requires confirmation, and the UI rejects concurrent execution while an action or approval is pending. Ambiguous sequential network retries remain a residual risk of backend endpoints without idempotency keys. | MITIGATED (partial) |
| TM-WEB-018 | Phishing link or transcript flooding via provider-supplied attestation metadata | Medium | `provider_attestation_required` (EVE-952) promotes a URL and gate types out of the provider's error body into prose the reader is told to trust, so a hostile or compromised provider endpoint — including a user-configured OpenAI-compatible base URL — controls that metadata. `ChatErrorAlert` renders the turn-failure path as plain auto-escaped React text (no markdown, no autolink); other agent-message renderers may interpret markup. `attestation_confirm_url` (`crates/contracts/src/user_facing_error.rs`) accepts only `^https?://` (so `javascript:`/`data:`/`file:` can never become the link) and drops any URL over 300 characters rather than truncating it, falling back to OpenRouter's own settings page. Gate types are capped at 8 entries of 64 characters and restricted to ASCII identifier characters, preventing provider-controlled Markdown, Slack mrkdwn links and mentions, and control characters from entering platform-authored prose. The confirmation host is deliberately not pinned: the page is not always on the API host, and the residual risk is a link to an attacker-chosen HTTPS page, not script execution. `MarkdownLink` rejects non-http(s) schemes independently (mirrors TM-WEB-012), and the public surface never sees this code at all — `PublicError::from_internal_code` maps it to `service_unavailable`. | MITIGATED |
| TM-WEB-019 | Host-header SSRF and cookie leak from server-rendered UI prefetch | High | Server-rendered pages (agents, harnesses, sessions, skills) prefetch API data and forward the browser's cookies. The origin previously came from the request `Host`, which any client chooses: the reference Caddy configs route every unmatched Host to the UI, and the UI proxy only checks that an auth cookie is present, so a crafted request could aim cookie-bearing fetches at an arbitrary or internal service, and an attacker HTTPS origin could redirect further inward. `resolveTrustedApiBaseUrl` (`apps/ui/src/lib/server-query.ts`) now takes the API base only from deployment configuration (`UI_SERVER_API_URL`, else `PUBLIC_APP_URL` + `/api`), never from request headers; with neither configured it fails closed and skips prefetch. Fetches use `redirect: "manual"` and treat any redirect as failure. Ingress Host filtering is therefore not required for this path. Regression: `apps/ui/src/__tests__/server-query.test.ts` (EVE-1150). | MITIGATED |

### Mitigation Details

**TM-WEB-004 / TM-WEB-005, Security Headers (MITIGATED):**
Applied via `SetResponseHeaderLayer` (`if_not_present`) in `app_builder.rs`:
- `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, prevents clickjacking
- `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, prevents MIME sniffing
- `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, limits referrer leakage
- `Permissions-Policy` disables unused browser device APIs. Default: `camera=(), microphone=(), geolocation=()`. When the `voice` feature flag is enabled, microphone is narrowed to same-origin use: `camera=(), microphone=(self), geolocation=()`.
- `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data: blob:; connect-src 'self'; font-src 'self'; frame-src 'self' data:; frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'self'; form-action 'self'` (`frame-src` permits the PDF preview `data:` iframe, TM-WEB-011, while keeping `about:srcdoc` previews under `'self'`)

## 13. AI Agent Behavior (TM-AGENT)

The agent loop is a core trust boundary: an LLM decides which tools to call with what arguments. The system prompt, user messages, tool results, and MCP tool descriptions all influence LLM behavior. Agents are semi-trusted within organizational scope, the agent creator (org member) is trusted, but the LLM's runtime decisions are not fully controllable.

| ID | Threat | Severity | Mitigation | Status |
|----|--------|----------|------------|--------|
| TM-AGENT-001 | Direct prompt injection via user message | High | Role separation (user vs system); LLM providers apply safety training; no complete defense | **ACCEPTED** |
| TM-AGENT-002 | Indirect prompt injection via tool results | High | Tool results use `tool_result` role, not `system`; LLM may still follow adversarial instructions in results | **ACCEPTED** |
| TM-AGENT-003 | Indirect prompt injection via MCP tool descriptions | Medium | MCP tool names/descriptions fed to LLM as tool schema; adversarial descriptions could influence behavior | **ACCEPTED** |
| TM-AGENT-004 | Agent jailbreak via system prompt | Medium | System prompt set by org member at agent creation; no sanitization of prompt content | **BY DESIGN** |
| TM-AGENT-005 | Capability escalation via agent creation | High | RiskLevel enum on Capability trait; high-risk capabilities (`a2a_agent_delegation`, `ag_ui_delegation`, `docker_container`, `daytona`, `e2b`, `deno`, `bashkit_shell`, `web_fetch`, `model_scout`) require Admin role to assign via API; the same gate covers effective parent/dependency capabilities when assigning a harness to an agent or directly selecting one for a session; running an already-configured agent remains permitted, and member-owned agents that already had high-risk capabilities are grandfathered (see `knowledge/execution/capabilities.md` "Admin-Only Tier Decision") | MITIGATED |
| TM-AGENT-006 | Cost runaway, unbounded LLM calls | High | Max iterations per turn (default 100); configurable per agent | MITIGATED |
| TM-AGENT-007 | Cost runaway, many tools per iteration | Medium | No per-iteration tool call limit; agent can invoke many tools in a single LLM response | **OPEN** |
| TM-AGENT-008 | Context window poisoning | Medium | Auto-compaction via `llm_driver.compact()` on `RequestTooLarge`; older messages compressed | MITIGATED |
| TM-AGENT-009 | Agent self-modification | Medium | Agents with `platform` can modify agents/sessions via tools; the capability must be explicitly assigned and is org-scoped | **OPEN** |
| TM-AGENT-010 | Agent spawning agent chains | Medium | Agents with `platform` can create agents/sessions; the capability must be explicitly assigned; no recursive depth limit | **OPEN** |
| TM-AGENT-011 | Sensitive data in system prompt | Medium | PII must not be placed in system prompts; no encryption at rest for prompts | **OPEN** |
| TM-AGENT-012 | Tool result size amplification | Medium | 64 KiB hard limit on tool results via `OutputHardLimitHook` (EVE-225); always-on final hook in ActAtom | MITIGATED |
| TM-AGENT-013 | Exfiltration via web_fetch | Medium | Agent with web_fetch capability can send session data to arbitrary URLs | **ACCEPTED** |
| TM-AGENT-014 | Confused deputy, tool call with wrong session | Low | Tool context includes session_id; tools scoped to active session only | MITIGATED |
| TM-AGENT-015 | Dangling or concurrently resolved tool calls cause LLM confusion | Low | User messages, client results, URL consent, and timeouts compete through one atomic parked-turn claim. Only the claim holder can persist while competing callers receive a conflict. Expired claims are reclaimed only by the same resolver kind or by the recovery sweep, so a different action cannot acknowledge discarded input. Ask User answers bind only to the newest requested tool-call batch, so a stale card cannot resolve a later pause. A user message cancels pending calls before resuming, durable resume is awaited before the session becomes active, and event or durable-enqueue failures restore the waiting state for retry. Transcript repair synthesizes any other missing results before an LLM call. | MITIGATED |
| TM-AGENT-016 | Plaintext secrets in chat history | Medium | `ask_user` with `kind: "secret"` gives an agent an in-flow way to collect a credential that never reaches the event log: the client stores the value through the encrypted session-secret endpoint and the tool result carries only a `session:{name}` reference, with no `value` field on the answer type to put one in. Prompt guidance names it as the affordance instead of asking in prose. Does not stop a user pasting a key into an ordinary chat message; it removes the reason to | MITIGATED (partial, in-flow only) |
| TM-AGENT-017 | Agent-initiated entity management | High | `platform` exposes the full scriptable command catalog. It is high-risk and explicitly assigned. Members may use the immutable managed assistant on the built-in Generic harness without attaching any new capabilities; assigning arbitrary high-risk capabilities still requires admin authority. Each call uses the session owner's real caller and active permission resolver; only that owner may submit Platform Chat turns, fork its conversations, change or fire its session schedules, or discover/execute its context-aware slash commands. Protection follows the managed built-in Agent identity rather than its harness name, and the distributed adapter reloads the org-scoped session without accepting a worker-supplied user identity. Runtime delegation responses are projected server-side by [`runtime_command_view`](../../crates/server/src/services/runtime_command_view.rs) after the same command authorization; every inherited harness ancestor is also resolved through that org-scoped command context. Unsupported response projections are rejected before any command runs. No fine-grained ownership RBAC exists within commands the caller is otherwise allowed to use. | **OPEN** |
| TM-AGENT-018 | Outbound URL filtering on web_fetch | Medium | Per-layer `NetworkAccessList` (harness ∩ agent ∩ session, narrow-only merge) plus optional deployment-wide system allowlist, both enforced at the `EgressService` boundary; web_fetch routes through egress with per-redirect-hop re-validation | MITIGATED |
| TM-AGENT-019 | Internal network probing via high-risk execution capabilities | High | `daytona` and `e2b` provide full network access by design; `docker_container` uses host networking in dev mode; all rely on Admin-only assignment plus infrastructure egress isolation | **ACCEPTED** |
| TM-AGENT-020 | Cross-session resource reuse via stale or guessed external IDs | Critical | Provider-owned resource IDs are checked against the active session's leased-resource/session-resource ownership before tool execution; raw sandbox list endpoints are filtered to owned IDs only | MITIGATED |
| TM-AGENT-021 | System prompt regurgitation | Medium | Opt-in `prompt_canary_guardrail` capability runs a streaming output guardrail that replaces the assistant message when the first sentence of the system prompt appears verbatim in the model output; original tokens are dropped and never persisted. Catches verbatim leaks only, paraphrased or partial leaks pass through. See `knowledge/execution/capabilities.md` § Output Guardrails | MITIGATED (partial, opt-in) |
| TM-AGENT-022 | Agent-initiated machine-payment spend | High | Paid capabilities cannot directly sign or submit arbitrary paid HTTP requests. They call `PaymentAuthority`, which selects an active policy matching the session/agent/agent identity/user/org, capability, target host, rail, and per-request limit before signing; a `user` policy matches only the user proven to have interactively sent the turn's input, so unattended turns never inherit the owner's wallet; attempts are audited | MITIGATED |
| TM-AGENT-023 | Redirect-based payment host-allowlist bypass | High | The payment authority validates only the original request URL host against the active policy's `allowed_hosts`. The outbound `reqwest` client used by `ServerPaymentAuthority::send_http_request` disables redirect following (`redirect::Policy::none()`), so 30x responses cannot be used to redirect a paid request to an unvalidated/internal host or downgrade from HTTPS | MITIGATED |
| TM-AGENT-024 | A2A outbound delegation egress / SSRF bypass | High | Outbound A2A delegation respects the merged `ToolContext.network_access` ACL. `enforce_network_access` (in `crates/capabilities/src/capabilities/a2a_delegation/`) validates the configured `base_url` and every resolved `AgentCard` interface URL against the runtime ACL before the A2A client is built. Discovery and every interface request additionally run `validate_url_dns_pinned` (or a controlled resolver in tests), pin the resolved addresses into the HTTP client, and disable redirects so DNS rebinding or a 30x cannot move the request into private/loopback/link-local/metadata space. `allow_local_urls` (default false) relaxes the address check only when `DEPLOYMENT_GRADE=dev`; other grades reject the hatch. `submit_run`, `wait_for_run`, and `cancel_task` all flow through this gate (EVE-1173) | MITIGATED |
| TM-AGENT-027 | Untrusted A2A structured artifact bypasses the parent result contract | Medium | Schema-bound external delegation takes only the first terminal A2A data part, validates it with the same delegation-result validator used for local child reports, and persists it only after conformance. Missing data fails `no_result`; invalid data fails `schema_mismatch`; neither is exposed as a successful task result. `message_schema` is rejected because remote agents cannot receive the local progress-reporting tool. | MITIGATED |
| TM-AGENT-026 | Exfiltration / web reach via OpenRouter provider-executed server tools | High | The opt-in `openrouter_server_tools` capability (`crates/integrations/src/openrouter/src/server_tools.rs`) lets the model invoke OpenRouter server tools (`web_search`, `web_fetch`, etc.). OpenRouter executes them **provider-side**, so Everruns' `NetworkAccessList`/`EgressService` egress controls (TM-AGENT-018) do **not** apply, same exfil class as TM-AGENT-013 but the egress boundary is OpenRouter's, not ours. Mitigations: capability is `RiskLevel::High` (admin-only assignment) and must be explicitly enabled per agent; only known server tools serialize (closed enum, unknown names dropped); no Everruns infra reach, secrets, or local execution are exposed. Residual cross-provider egress is the operator's OpenRouter-account responsibility | **ACCEPTED** |
| TM-AGENT-029 | Exfiltration / web reach / code execution via OpenAI hosted tools | High | The opt-in `openai_server_tools` capability (`crates/core/src/builtins/openai_server_tools.rs`) lets the model invoke OpenAI hosted `web_search`, `code_interpreter`, `shell` (code execution in an OpenAI container) `file_search` (over operator-listed OpenAI vector stores) and remote `mcp` (operator-listed servers), which OpenAI executes **provider-side**, sending conversation content to OpenAI-side tools and tool arguments to the listed MCP servers. Everruns' egress controls (TM-AGENT-018) do not apply. Mitigations: `RiskLevel::High` (admin-only assignment), explicit per-agent enablement, closed tool list validated on write, optional domain allow-list, remote MCP approval defaulting to `always` (each call pauses the turn for a person; `never` only with an explicit `allowed_tools` list, and only `{"approve": true}` approves), `https` MCP URLs without userinfo so no credential sits in agent config; servers needing credentials are referenced by registered name (`mcp_server`) and resolved per call from the MCP connection and secret stores under the session's own scoping, below the engine, so headers never reach events and `Debug` redacts them; a missing grant fails the turn instead of sending an unauthenticated request, and the request is refused on any provider that is not OpenAI or Azure OpenAI. Residual egress is the operator's OpenAI-account responsibility | **ACCEPTED** |
| TM-AGENT-028 | Model-triggered detached session creation exceeds caller authority | High | The model can request detached spawning but cannot provide an authorization identity or budget root. `ToolContext` receives both from the host: the resolved session owner must satisfy `SESSION_MANAGE`, and the returned root is org-validated control-plane metadata. Authorization runs before session creation. | MITIGATED |
| TM-AGENT-030 | AG-UI outbound delegation egress / SSRF bypass | High | The model selects a configured agent id from the admin-set `ag_ui_delegation` config and never supplies a URL. `build_client` (in `crates/capabilities/src/capabilities/ag_ui_delegation/run.rs`) runs before every request, including each resume: it checks the endpoint against the merged `ToolContext.network_access` ACL (the spawn-time policy is stored with the run and restored for resumes), re-validates the config, refuses loopback, private and metadata addresses with `validate_url_dns_pinned` and pins the resolved addresses into the client, and disables redirects so a 30x cannot move the request or its bearer token. `allow_local_urls` (default false) relaxes the address check only for tests/dev | MITIGATED |
| TM-AGENT-031 | Untrusted AG-UI stream content (prompt injection, malformed or oversized streams, forged result) | Medium | The remote stream passes the `everruns-core` (`ag-ui` feature) consumer pipeline: 1.0 sequencing rules, unknown members stripped, a 4 MiB per-event cap, and the first violation fails the run. Stored text, conversation history and interrupts are bounded (`MAX_RESULT_CHARS`, `MAX_HISTORY_MESSAGES`, `MAX_INTERRUPTS`), and remote text reaches the parent model only as a tool result or task summary (same class as TM-AGENT-002). A `result_schema` is checked against the `RUN_FINISHED` result with the shared delegation-result validator; missing or non-conforming results fail `no_result` / `schema_mismatch`. `message_schema` is rejected | MITIGATED |
| TM-AGENT-032 | AG-UI delegation credential exposure | High | The bearer token lives in the encrypted session secret store and the config holds only its name (`bearer_token_secret`); `Authorization`, `Proxy-Authorization` and `Cookie` are refused as static headers. The value is read when each run starts, sent as a sensitive header, and never written to the run record, the session task, the result artifact or the tool result (asserted by `foreground_spawn_streams_text_back_with_the_bearer_secret`) | MITIGATED |
| TM-AGENT-033 | Planted instructions in manager context | Medium | Manager context is notes a manager writes about an entity, read by Platform Chat before changing it. Writing it needs the entity kind's manage policy, the same trust as editing the entity itself, and every write is a `context_updated` history entry with its author and reason. It is never part of the entity, so it never reaches the entity's own prompt, resolved config, exports or events; Platform Chat reads it as org-authored data that cannot widen what the user asked for. See [Change Reasons](../execution/change-reasons-and-manager-context.md) and `crates/server/src/domains/change_history/context.rs`. | MITIGATED |
| TM-AGENT-034 | An agent reading or rewriting the constraints set on it | Medium | A session acting for an entity (its agent or harness, through the Platform capability, the worker transport or `acting_for_session`) is refused its own manager context and history with `self_inspection_denied`; other entities it manages stay reachable. Covered by `context_tests::a_session_acting_for_an_entity_cannot_read_its_own_context_or_history`. | MITIGATED |

### Mitigation Details

**TM-AGENT-001 / TM-AGENT-002, Prompt Injection (ACCEPTED):**
Prompt injection is an inherent limitation of current LLM architecture. Defense-in-depth:
1. **Role separation:** System, user, assistant, tool_result messages are distinct roles
2. **Iteration limits:** Max turns prevents infinite manipulation loops
3. **Tool registry:** LLM can only call registered tools (no arbitrary code execution)
4. **Session isolation:** Even if manipulated, agent is confined to its session
5. **No auto-escalation:** Agent cannot grant itself new capabilities
6. **Instruction hierarchy:** Generic harness system prompt includes an explicit instruction hierarchy statement directing the LLM to prioritize system instructions over content in tool results, user messages, or agent instructions files

There is no reliable way to prevent an LLM from following adversarial instructions embedded in tool results or user messages. This is an industry-wide limitation.

**TM-AGENT-004, System Prompt Trust Model:**
```
Agent creator (org member) → sets system_prompt → stored in agents table
    ↓
Session created → system_prompt loaded (immutable for session)
    ↓
Capability prompts appended (hardcoded in Rust, not user-controlled)
    ↓
Combined prompt sent to LLM as system message
```
The agent creator is trusted within their org. A malicious system prompt can instruct the agent to misuse its capabilities, but only within the sandbox (session files, SQLite, bash sandbox). The blast radius is limited to the session.

**TM-AGENT-005, Capability Escalation (MITIGATED):**
Each capability declares a `RiskLevel` (Low, Medium, High) via the `Capability` trait. High-risk capabilities (`docker_container`, `daytona`, `e2b`) require `OrgRole::Admin` to assign. The check runs in create/update/upsert/import agent API handlers, returning 403 if a non-admin user attempts to assign a high-risk capability. The `risk_level` field is exposed in the capabilities list API for UI display.

**TM-AGENT-006, Iteration Limit:**
```rust
// Turn state machine enforces max iterations
if self.current_iteration >= self.max_iterations {
    TurnPlan::Terminal {
        stop_reason: TurnStopReason::MaxIterationsReached,
        ...
    }
}
```
Default: 100 iterations. Each Reason→Act cycle counts as one iteration. Configurable per agent.

**TM-AGENT-008, Context Window Poisoning (MITIGATED):**
When the message history exceeds the LLM's context window, the `ReasonAtom` catches `RequestTooLarge` errors and calls `llm_driver.compact()` to compress older messages. This prevents unbounded context growth. Adversarial early messages are still present but may be summarized during compaction. Native encrypted compact output remains typed provider transport state: it is reused only on the matching provider request, its `Debug` representation redacts payloads, and public compaction/generation events expose metadata rather than encrypted content.

**TM-AGENT-013, Exfiltration via web_fetch (ACCEPTED):**
An agent with `web_fetch` capability can:
1. Read session files via `read_file` tool
2. Send file contents to external URL via `web_fetch` tool

This is accepted because:
- Agent capabilities are chosen by org members (trusted)
- `web_fetch` is an opt-in capability, not default
- The intended use case requires external HTTP access
- Removing this would break legitimate functionality

**TM-AGENT-016, Plaintext Secrets in Chat History (MITIGATED, partial, in-flow only):**
When an agent tool (e.g., Daytona) doesn't find an API key, it may instruct the user to provide one in chat. The user types the key as a chat message, which is stored as plaintext in the `events` table (message content). The `session_secrets` table encrypts the value separately, but the original chat message retains plaintext indefinitely.

- **Impact:** API keys visible in session history, event exports, and any observability pipeline that captures events.
- **Current mitigations:** `ask_user` with `kind: "secret"` (EVE-1058) is the in-flow alternative that previously did not exist, which is what made "phase out in-chat secret collection" unfollowable. An `ask_user` answer is a *tool result*, so a credential typed into an ordinary question box would have been strictly worse than one typed in chat — plaintext in the event log **and** replayed into model context every turn. The secret kind is built so that cannot happen: `AskUserAnswer` has no `value` field at all (see `crates/core/src/builtins/ask_user.rs`), the client stores the value through the already-encrypted `PUT /v1/sessions/{id}/storage/secrets` path and then answers with a `session:{name}` reference, and `validate_answers` in `crates/server/src/api/question_answers.rs` refuses an answer that carries a selection or free text on a secret question rather than ignoring it. Free text and multi-select are normalized off the question for the same reason. The batch also never auto-resolves: a "default credential" is meaningless, so both `DefaultsResponder` and the engine's unattended path decline it, leaving "proceed without the credential" as the model's explicit decision.
  The other door is closed too (EVE-1059). An ordinary choice question with `allow_other: true` or a `text` question produces free text, and that text *is* a tool result, so the secret kind alone would have left `ask_user` opening this door wider than chat does.
